 Internal · TRAFFIK · Not for distribution 
 Edwards Lifesciences PASCAL Global Campaign finalist brief 
 Edwards' Transcatheter Mitral and Tricuspid Therapies (TMTT) team wants one agency partner to build a new, multi-year global campaign for PASCAL, its TEER platform for mitral and tricuspid repair. The goal: make PASCAL the brand operators think of first for TEER, in a category where Abbott's MitraClip is the habit. The proposal (PowerPoint) is due Mon Nov 10 . We present it to the TMTT team on Fri Nov 13 · 9:45 AM PT on Teams. Selection is the week of Nov 16, kickoff Nov 16, in market April 2027. Vanessa's invitation also says TMTT has two more global campaigns planned and the PASCAL partner may get those too.

 00B Primer: Edwards, PASCAL and this project in plain words 
 For anyone new to Edwards or heart devices. Read this before the rest of the brief. It covers what Edwards sells and to whom, what PASCAL does, the campaigns around it, and exactly what this project is asking us to build. 
 1 · Who Edwards is 
 Edwards Lifesciences is a medical device company headquartered at One Edwards Way in Irvine, a short drive from our office. It focuses on structural heart disease : problems with the heart's valves and structure rather than its arteries. 
 Its history goes back to the first commercially successful artificial heart valve (Lowell Edwards and Dr. Albert Starr, 1960). It became an independent company when it spun off from Baxter in 2000. 
 Today it has three main heart businesses, and we have worked in all three:
 TAVR / THV (transcatheter heart valves): the SAPIEN family, which replaces a narrowed aortic valve through a catheter instead of open-heart surgery. This is Edwards' biggest business and where most of our past work sits (Lifetime Management, S3UR, Pulmonic, TCT, EuroPCR). 
 Surgical: valves surgeons sew in during open-heart surgery (INSPIRIS RESILIA, MITRIS RESILIA, KONECT). We did the MITRIS brochure. 
 TMTT (Transcatheter Mitral and Tricuspid Therapies): the newest and fastest-growing unit (+47% in Q2 2026). It treats the two valves that used to need surgery or had no good option. This pitch is for TMTT. 
 2 · The medical basics, in plain words 
 The two valves The mitral valve sits on the left side of the heart, between the upper and lower chambers. The tricuspid valve is the same job on the right side. Each has flaps (leaflets) that open to let blood through and close to stop it flowing back. 
 What goes wrong Regurgitation means the valve leaks: the leaflets don't close fully and blood flows backward. Mitral regurgitation is “MR,” tricuspid is “TR.” Patients get breathless, tired and swollen, and it can lead to heart failure. Many are older or too sick for open-heart surgery. 
 Repair vs. replace Doctors can repair the patient's own valve or replace it with a new one. Both can now be done through a catheter, a thin tube that goes in through a vein in the leg and up to the heart, with no chest opening. 
 What TEER is TEER (transcatheter edge-to-edge repair) is the main repair option. A small implant is guided up to the heart and grabs the two leaking leaflet edges and holds them together, so the leak gets smaller. “M-TEER” is mitral, “T-TEER” is tricuspid. 
 3 · Who actually chooses the device 
 The operator: an interventional cardiologist who does the procedure in the cath lab. The RFP calls them “TEER operators.” They are the core audience. 
 The imager: a cardiologist who reads the ultrasound images during the procedure and tells the operator whether the leaflets are grasped well. Their confidence matters a lot. 
 The heart team: the operator, imager, cardiac surgeon, heart-failure doctor and nurse coordinator who decide together how to treat each patient. 
 Who this campaign is not for: patients and consumers. Edwards sells the device to hospitals, and the doctors choose which system their program uses. Everything here is HCP (healthcare professional) marketing , and paid media isn't part of the budget. 
 How they're reached: Edwards sales reps and clinical specialists who stand in the cath lab; big medical conferences (TCT, EuroPCR, ACC, London Valves); and digital journeys (account-based marketing, HCP email, web, social) between visits. 
 4 · The TMTT products 
 Product What it does Valve Main competitor 
 PASCAL Precision (implants: PASCAL and the smaller PASCAL Ace) Repair (TEER). Grabs and holds the leaflets together. Each side can be grasped on its own, and a central spacer helps fill the gap. Capture Clarity adds markers on the X-ray that confirm when a leaflet has been grasped. Mitral and tricuspid Abbott MitraClip (mitral) and TriClip (tricuspid) 
 EVOQUE Replacement (TTVR). A whole new tricuspid valve, delivered by catheter. Tricuspid No transcatheter tricuspid replacement from Abbott 
 SAPIEN M3 Replacement (TMVR). A new mitral valve, delivered by catheter. Mitral — 
 Edwards says it is the only company with both repair and replacement through a catheter for both the mitral and tricuspid valves in the US. That's why its campaigns never pit one Edwards device against another. 
 5 · The competitive picture 
 Abbott got there first. MitraClip was the first TEER device and has been in US programs since 2013. TriClip was the first US tricuspid TEER device (2024). Many hospitals built their TEER program around Abbott, so MitraClip is the habit. 
 PASCAL came second (US approval for degenerative mitral regurgitation in 2022; Europe earlier). It earned a reputation as the device for difficult, complex anatomy. That's a compliment, but it also keeps PASCAL out of routine cases. 
 What changed on Oct 21, 2026: the FDA approved PASCAL for tricuspid in the US and approved Capture Clarity for mitral. A next-generation system for both valves is coming. That's why Edwards wants a new campaign now. 
 6 · The Edwards campaigns around this one 
 What each one sells and to whom, so the team knows where PASCAL fits. 
 Campaign Unit What it sells, in one line Our role 
 TMTT Portfolio Campaign : “Patient-matched &amp; Advancing with every case” (soft-launched TCT 2026) TMTT The umbrella: Edwards has a repair or replacement option matched to every mitral and tricuspid patient. PASCAL is meant to be the hero under it. None; we have the XLT draft 
 PASCAL Global Campaign (this pitch) TMTT Make PASCAL the first TEER brand operators think of, on both valves, for years. Pitching

 7 · This project: goals and objectives 
 The goal, in Edwards' words 
 “Make PASCAL the brand operators think of first for TEER across mitral and tricuspid, today and moving forward.” Success means PASCAL becomes “the default, not just the alternative.” 
 The four beliefs to change 
 “Abbott is the established name in TEER” → “PASCAL is leading where TEER is going.” 
 “PASCAL is the device for complex anatomy” → “PASCAL is my first choice for TEER.” 
 “Treating the other valve means a new learning curve” → “My PASCAL experience carries across mitral and tricuspid.” 
 “Changing my default costs my team time and effort” → “Edwards makes moving to PASCAL straightforward.” 
 Where the growth is Mostly inside hospitals that already use PASCAL some of the time : move operators from occasional to routine use, and get accounts using it on one valve to use it on the other. Then select programs that are new to PASCAL. Focus markets: interventional cardiologists in the US and Europe , with Japan and other regions using the toolkit. 
 What they want built One lasting campaign idea and visual system ; a messaging framework with tailored messages for each audience segment; key visuals and a style guide; and a launch toolkit and activation playbook the regions can run. It has to stay fresh as new evidence, indications and devices arrive, “not a short launch flight.” 
 How they'll measure it PASCAL's share of TEER inside priority accounts, by valve and region; operators moving from occasional to routine use; second-valve adoption; and how operators describe PASCAL: a first choice, not only a complex-anatomy device. 
 $200–400K Agency work only, no paid media. Show what $200K buys and what $400K adds 
 Nov 16 Kickoff; selection the week of Nov 16 
 Apr 2027 In market, subject to approvals 
 4 phases Discover and define · Create · Build and launch · Evolve 
 8 · Words you'll hear 
 Term Meaning 
 TEER · M-TEER · T-TEER Transcatheter edge-to-edge repair · on the mitral valve · on the tricuspid valve 
 MR · TR · DMR Mitral regurgitation · tricuspid regurgitation · degenerative (primary) MR, where the valve itself is worn or damaged 
 TMVR · TTVR · TAVR Transcatheter mitral / tricuspid / aortic valve replacement 
 Operator · imager · heart team The doctor doing the procedure · the doctor reading the images · the group deciding treatment 
 Leaflet grasp / capture The moment the implant catches the valve flaps. Capture Clarity confirms it on screen. 
 CLASP IITR The tricuspid PASCAL trial whose one-year results came out at TCT 2026 
 KCCQ A standard patient quality-of-life score for heart failure 
 MLR · Vault · claims matrix Medical, legal and regulatory review · Edwards' review system · the list of approved claims and their references 
 HCP · KOL · ABX Healthcare professional · key opinion leader · account-based experience (marketing aimed at specific hospitals) 
 TCT · EuroPCR · ACC · London Valves The big interventional cardiology conferences where Edwards launches news 
 CE Mark European approval. PASCAL has had it for tricuspid since 2020.

 02 Our story in one breath 
 PASCAL doesn't need to win an argument. It needs to become a habit. We're the partner already inside the Edwards brand, ready on November 16, with one lasting idea built in chapters so every piece of news makes it stronger. 
 Beat 1 · The real competitor is a habit Abbott got there first. MitraClip is the reflex: the device the team sets up for, the one the imager knows how to read, the one nobody has to defend. Features rarely break a reflex. 
 Beat 2 · Defaults are set by heart teams Four hypotheses: a default is a team decision; confidence is earned at the moment of capture; the complex-anatomy label is “a compliment in a cage”; the second valve is the easiest new habit. 
 Beat 3 · One idea, built in chapters Chapters, not flights. The tricuspid indication, Capture Clarity, the next-generation system and new evidence each renew the same promise instead of replacing it. 
 Beat 4 · No learning curve Twelve years with Edwards: the brand, MLR review (including VAULT), the congress calendar and the people. Our Irvine office is minutes from One Edwards Way. 
 Beat 5 · Transparent and built to grow $200K foundation, $400K full, priced in modules, with fees separate from third-party costs and no markup. Ready to extend to TMTT's next two campaigns.

 05 PASCAL, TMTT and the TEER market 
 Oct 21 FDA approves PASCAL Precision for tricuspid TEER, and PASCAL Ace with Capture Clarity for degenerative mitral TEER 
 80% vs 30% CLASP IITR at TCT: moderate-or-less TR at one year with PASCAL vs. medical therapy; KCCQ +18 
 +47% TMTT Q2 2026 sales, $195.9M; 2026 guidance $760–780M 
 3 TMTT therapies: PASCAL Precision (TEER), EVOQUE (TTVR), SAPIEN M3 (TMVR) 
 The tricuspid news landed after the RFP was written. On Oct 21, 2026 the FDA approved PASCAL Precision for T-TEER: symptomatic severe TR despite optimal medical therapy, intermediate or higher surgical risk, heart-team judged. The same day it approved PASCAL Ace with Capture Clarity for degenerative mitral TEER, which Edwards calls “the first FDA-approved TEER device with fluoroscopic indicators that confirm when valve leaflets have been grasped.” Edwards now says it is “the only company to provide a portfolio of repair and replacement transcatheter therapies for the mitral and tricuspid valves in the United States.” 
 CLASP IITR one-year results (TCT, early Nov 2026): 80% of PASCAL patients at moderate or less TR at one year vs. 30% on medical therapy alone, with an 18-point quality-of-life gain on the KCCQ. Use only Edwards' approved wording in any creative. This is MLR territory. 
 Abbott: MitraClip was first in mitral, and TriClip was first in US tricuspid TEER. US operators now have two T-TEER options. Abbott has no transcatheter tricuspid replacement. 
 Leadership: Daveen Chopra is Corporate VP, TMTT, and was quoted on the approval. CEO Bernard Zovighian. Edwards expected the US tricuspid launch and Capture Clarity approval in Q4 (Q2 2026 results). 
 EU: PASCAL has had CE Mark for TR since Oct 2020, so Europe already uses it on both valves. That's the RFP's “fresh news for established markets.” 
 05A Our Edwards history and what it tells us 
 From the past Edwards files in our Drive (read Nov 4, 2026). Use this to answer “what do you already know about us?” and to keep the creative in bounds. 
 Most important: TMTT already has a portfolio campaign 
 The TMTT Portfolio Campaign (XLT draft, Oct 2026) soft-launched at TCT 2026 with the line “Patient-matched &amp; Advancing with every case” , three pillars ( Empower, Enable, Elevate ), four physician archetypes (including “Patient-Fit Strategist” and “Program Growth Builder”) and validation with 230+ clinicians in the US and EU. It says the portfolio campaign “supports PASCAL TR as the hero” , that “the portfolio supports. It never competes” and that “therapy campaigns stay hero.” The PASCAL campaign has to sit under that umbrella. Show we know it, and that our territories plug into it. 
 2027 plan in that deck: journeys orchestrated on Adobe across paid media, ABX, social, web and email between field visits. Edwards' own numbers: 2.7× more case volume in accounts with ABX engagement (2025), and ABX reached 85% of PASCAL and 87% of EVOQUE target accounts this year. Cite these only as Edwards' figures. 
 We're already doing TMTT work 
 SAPIEN M3 (live): on Oct 11, 2026 Hana Su (Edwards, SAPIEN M3) asked us for three pillar visuals (Outcomes / Innovation / Legacy), modeled on the EVOQUE “REVOLUTIONARY” approach, for an animation and HCP print by mid-November under a new SOW. The email went to Savannah, who is leaving TRAFFIK. Make sure someone owns it now. 
 ENCIRCLE 2-year deck (SAPIEN M3, Vault draft, Sept 2026): 97% MR ≤1+ at 2 years, &lt;1% 30-day mortality, KCCQ +17. Asset PP--US-11823. 
 PASCAL has been on our creative already: the EuroPCR 2024 wall featured PASCAL Precision mitral and tricuspid and EVOQUE. Our TCT 2024 “NEW FRONTIERS” work (SLT-approved pillars Innovation / Evidence / Partnership) included PASCAL, PASCAL ACE and EVOQUE. 
 Mitral: MITRIS surgical brochure and mitral valve-in-valve (MViV) work with SAPIEN. 
 How Edwards builds a global campaign (EVOQUE and Pulmonic) 
 Global sets a three-pillar platform with modular claims and pillar imagery. EVOQUE: Heart = Innovation, Dial = Evidence, Patient = Outcome. Its lockup can't be changed, and every claim is footnoted. 
 Global writes the style guide and templates (Webdam / SharePoint). Regions adapt them, and translations go through Global. 
 Separate executions for personal (rep) and non-personal channels, and separate US and EU legal versions (PP--US / PP--EU codes). 
 EVOQUE messaging stays “without going head-to-head against T-TEER.” By extension, PASCAL creative should treat repair and replacement as working together and never undercut Edwards' own devices. 
 How Edwards plans and positions (THV files from 2023–24) 
 A launch and a platform, never merged. For S3UR and Lifetime Management, Edwards ran a product launch and an evergreen platform side by side: S3UR was “the proof point for a larger promise,” and the two would “never ‘integrate’ into one creative concept.” Rollout went region by region (US, Japan, EU, then the rest), timed to congresses and “depending on approval.” Our chapters model fits this: PASCAL news renews the platform instead of becoming a new campaign. 
 Their own diagnosis of short flights: “Implanter campaigns are typically deployed in short flights with discrete messaging.” The fix they chose was a “surround sound” story with hand-offs from message to message. Quote it back when we say “chapters, not flights.” 
 Positioning frame we've used with them: To / Who / Is / That / Because / So that, then a claims matrix, then “socialize final positioning statement with regions.” Recurring words: predictable, durable, partner of choice. 
 Planning language: BU strategy, then goals, objectives and strategies; programs on one template (problem, tactics, success criteria, budget, timeline, dependencies, team). Goals are perception shifts tied to share (“advance perception of EW as the leader… by x% to protect/grow our share by y%”). KPIs run from campaign metrics to physician-level data (reach, frequency, engagement) to share. 
 Channels around the rep: Showpad, rep-triggered email, P2P, KOL video, podcasts, ABX pages by share level, and digital as “air cover” for the field. Congresses lead with the platform story to match the podium. 
 How they review and what they push back on 
 Process: working “cutting room floor” sessions, then R1 marketing reviewers, then R2 senior approver, then Vault MLR. Global campaigns need a global product and a global digital reviewer in Vault. Plan about 3 creative rounds plus 2 after Vault. TCT 2024 was scoped for 5 rounds with 2 held for Vault. 
 Pushback: color drift (“Red needs to be less orange, closer to Edwards red”; “Black isn't an approved color – dark grey”), trademark and legal handling (“Delete TM”; legal on a separate asset), secondary graphics at the wrong size, and “Back to the OG!” when a design went too far. 
 What they liked: ideas they could approve and extend (the Pulmonic “Unique Heart”, NEW FRONTIERS, the S3UR style guide) and stories turned into sales tools. Senior approvers wanted “beyond what we've done”: classy and futuristic, “not sci-fi.” 
 Watch these 
 No TRAFFIK performance numbers on file. The orientation deck says there are “no verified booth traffic, lead or ROI measures.” Don't invent results. Use client quotes, approvals (SLT-approved), scale (70 × 70 ft booth, global rollout) and Edwards' own figures, attributed to them. 
 2024 notes record that “TMTT … used another agency” for its booth, and list other agencies around Edwards: IPG, Relevate, Evoke, Focus, Authentica, Herbst. Any of them could be pitching. Never name them in the room. 
 None of our past files mention PASCAL's competitors: Abbott, MitraClip and TriClip never appear. The THV files are all about Medtronic (“keep Medtronic at bay”). Our Abbott read comes from the RFP and public sources. 
 The THV planning files also name Evoke, HB and Relevate, and the 2024 media plan was IPG Health's. Other agencies are close to Edwards. 
 Edwards names in our files

(media and style guide budget), Mitch (LTM demo), Michelle Harbaugh (Pulmonic differentiators deep dive, 2024), Kelly Fidler (THV director; on our advisory board).

Position → message → create → roll out, plus a toolkit for local teams. The same chain PASCAL asks for.

 Global: JAPAC sales training in Mandarin and Japanese; S3UR EU launch; Balt (Europe, APAC, LatAm); HID Global (100+ countries). 
 Medtech beyond Edwards: CathWorks FFRangio (coronary physiology for interventional specialists), Balt (neurovascular), Apria. 
 Gap to close: the RFP asks for case studies “with results.” Our two case studies show process and craft but no numbers. Add any results we can cite (adoption, usage, engagement, award, client quote), or be ready to say why medtech results are confidential.

 Strategy and the idea (8) Strategic thinking 25 and creative 20. Everyone should be able to answer these. 
 1 Why TRAFFIK for PASCAL? Brent Testing All criteria. Answer We&#x27;re already inside the Edwards brand: twelve years across THV, surgical and pulmonic, under MLR and VAULT every day, minutes from One Edwards Way. We can start on November 16 without a learning curve, and we bring one idea built in chapters so every piece of PASCAL news makes it stronger. Avoid Generic agency claims. 
 2 What is the real problem PASCAL has? Brent Testing Strategic thinking 25. Answer It isn&#x27;t features. MitraClip got there first and became the habit: the device the team sets up for and the imager knows how to read. PASCAL is respected but often filed as the complex-anatomy device. Our job is to move it from the alternative to the default, case by case and team by team. Avoid Knocking Abbott. 
 3 What are the four beliefs you want to move? Brent Testing Strategy. Answer From “PASCAL is for hard anatomy” to “first choice for TEER”; from “MitraClip is what my team knows” to “PASCAL is easy to adopt”; from “two valves, two decisions” to “one platform for both”; and from “a device” to “a platform that&#x27;s leading where TEER is going.” Phase 1 tests each one with operators and imagers. Avoid Presenting them as proven. 
 4 What would you want to explore further? Brent Testing RFP §9 item 2. Answer How heart teams set a default and who really decides; what “complex anatomy” means to operators, a badge or a box; how imagers feel at the moment of capture; and how mitral loyalists think about starting tricuspid. Those answers decide which territory we push. Avoid Saying we already know. 
 5 Walk us through the three territories. Jeremy Testing Creative 20. Answer Confidence, Captured: “The moment it holds, you know.” It owns the moment of capture, now made visible by Capture Clarity. Every Case: “From the hardest case to every case.” It turns the complex-anatomy label into a reason to choose PASCAL for everything. Fluent in Both: one platform and one skill set across mitral and tricuspid. Each one grows in chapters. Avoid Over-explaining the comps. 
 6 Which territory would you pick? Jeremy Testing Creative judgment. Answer We&#x27;d test all three, but Confidence, Captured lines up best with what&#x27;s new: Capture Clarity is approved, and confidence at capture is what an imager and an operator feel together. The test would tell us whether it travels as well in Europe, where PASCAL is already established on both valves. Avoid Refusing to have a view. 
 7 How does it stay fresh for years? Jeremy Testing RFP §5, keeping it fresh. Answer The idea stays constant and the chapters change: the tricuspid indication, Capture Clarity, the next-generation system, new evidence. Each chapter renews the same promise. That&#x27;s how we kept Lifetime Management running across congress cycles. Avoid Promising a new campaign every year. 
 8 How will you measure success? Amanda Testing RFP §5, measurement. Answer Three layers: business (PASCAL share in priority accounts by valve and region, occasional-to-routine use, second-valve adoption); perception (first choice vs. complex-anatomy device); engagement (toolkit use, congress, HCP email). Baseline in Phase 1, a quarterly dashboard, and pulses at six and twelve months in the full plan. Avoid Only vanity metrics. 
 PASCAL and TMTT specifics (5) The device, the indications, the portfolio campaign, the market. 
 9 How does PASCAL fit under the TMTT portfolio campaign? Brent Testing Portfolio fit. Answer The portfolio campaign, “Patient-matched &amp; Advancing with every case,” is the umbrella and PASCAL is meant to be the hero under it. Our idea plugs in as the PASCAL therapy story. It uses the same audiences and channels and never competes with EVOQUE or SAPIEN M3. Avoid Not knowing it exists. 
 10 How do you handle repair and replacement together? Jeremy Testing Portfolio sensitivity. Answer Edwards is the only company with both repair and replacement for mitral and tricuspid. PASCAL creative celebrates repair without ever undercutting EVOQUE or SAPIEN M3. The heart team picks the right therapy for the patient, and Edwards has it. Avoid Implying TEER beats replacement. 
 11 What changed with the October 21 approvals? Brent Testing Current facts. Answer The FDA approved PASCAL Precision for tricuspid TEER, and PASCAL Ace with Capture Clarity for degenerative mitral TEER. So tricuspid is now real in the US, and capture is now visible. That&#x27;s chapters one and two of our plan, live today. Avoid Calling them future. 
 12 How would this work globally? Darcey Testing Global scalability 15. Answer One global core with a toolkit that flexes by region: the US leads with tricuspid and Capture Clarity; Europe, where PASCAL has had tricuspid CE Mark since 2020, gets fresh news for an established brand; Japan and Asia Pacific get transcreated assets. We&#x27;ve done this with the Pulmonic toolkit, the S3UR EU launch and JAPAC training. Avoid Translation instead of transcreation. 
 13 How do you stay inside MLR? Jeremy Testing Regulated category. Answer We build the claims matrix early and use Edwards&#x27; approved wording only. Creative is shown early in working sessions, and we plan the VAULT rounds into the schedule. We keep US and EU versions separate where the labels differ. Avoid Promising speed over compliance. 
 By person in the room (5) What Vanessa, Eric, Yash, Ai Phi and Jackie are most likely to ask. 
 14 Vanessa: How would this grow when the next device arrives? Brent Testing Vanessa · brand lead. Answer It becomes the next chapter. The promise stays and the proof gets stronger, so the next-generation system arrives into a story people already know instead of starting over. Avoid A new campaign per device. 
 15 Eric: How will reps actually use this? Amanda Testing Eric · product. Answer A field toolkit built from the same idea: an in-service deck, a leave-behind, rep-triggered email and Showpad assets, modular so reps can match the account. We&#x27;d test them with a few reps before launch. Avoid Only big-brand assets. 
 16 Yash: What would change your recommendation? Brent Testing Yash · strategy. Answer If Phase 1 shows the default is set by the imager or the program rather than the operator, we&#x27;d shift the weight of the message and the media toward the heart team. The idea holds, but the emphasis moves. Avoid Defensiveness. 
 17 Ai Phi: How would regional teams adapt this without breaking it? Darcey Testing Ai Phi · marketing. Answer A style guide and templates with locked core elements and flexible local ones, plus regional sessions at launch. Translations go through Global, which is how Edwards already runs EVOQUE and Pulmonic. Avoid No guardrails. 
 18 Jackie: How would this work in our ABX and digital journeys? Amanda Testing Jackie · digital. Answer We&#x27;d build assets for digital from day one: ABX sequences, HCP email, paid social and web modules that plug into your Adobe journeys and dashboards. We measure with your data instead of building a separate system. Avoid Ignoring their stack.

 19 Budget numbers? Brent Testing Budget 10. Answer Foundation $199,600 ($167,600 fees, 976 hours, $32,000 third party). Full $398,680 ($287,680 fees, 1,702 hours, $111,000 third party), recommended. Rates $125 to $225, blended and held. Avoid Rounding wrongly. 
 20 Timeline? Brent Testing Plan. Answer Kickoff Nov 16, 2026. Discover and define to Jan 22; Create Jan–Mar; Build and launch Mar–Apr; in market April 2027; Evolve May–Dec 2027. Avoid Fuzzy dates. 
 21 Market facts? Brent Testing Context. Answer Approvals Oct 21, 2026; CLASP IITR one-year: 80% moderate or less TR vs. 30% on medical therapy, KCCQ +18 (Edwards&#x27; wording only); TMTT Q2 2026 $195.9M, +47%; 2026 guidance $760–780M. Avoid Using trial numbers in creative without MLR. 
 Hard ones (5) The ones that could cost us. Stay calm and stay inside the proposal. 
 22 Your case studies have no results. Why? Brent Testing Results gap. Answer Medtech results usually belong to the client and are confidential, so we show what we can: Edwards&#x27; own approvals, SLT sign-off, global rollout and scale. We&#x27;d be glad to have Edwards colleagues speak to the work. [Add any real number before Friday.] Avoid Inventing numbers. 
 23 Have you worked with any TEER or mitral/tricuspid competitor? Brent Testing Conflicts. Answer No. No work for Abbott or any other TEER, mitral or tricuspid maker in the past 24 months. Our mitral valve-in-valve SAPIEN work is for Edwards. Avoid Hedging. 
 24 You do a lot of THV work. Is TMTT going to get your A-team? Brent Testing Team. Answer Yes. The people on the invite are the team, and Amanda runs it day to day. THV experience is an advantage: we know the brand, the MLR process and the congress calendar already. Avoid Overpromising headcount. 
 25 Your timeline runs to December 2027. The RFP says Q2. Brent Testing Timeline mismatch. Answer The RFP says both: Q2 2027 in the table and end of 2027 in section 7. We planned to December so evolve is covered, and Phase 4 can move or come out without touching the launch. Avoid Telling them they&#x27;re wrong. 
 26 Isn&#x27;t “Every Case” a dig at MitraClip? Jeremy Testing Tone. Answer No. It&#x27;s about PASCAL&#x27;s range, not anyone else&#x27;s limits. We&#x27;re confident without attacking anyone, which is also what MLR expects. Avoid Naming Abbott. 
 Budget and ways of working (3) Brent leads. No new numbers in the room. 
 27 Can you do it for $300K? Brent Testing Budget flexibility. Answer Yes. It&#x27;s built in modules. We&#x27;d keep the tricuspid chapter kit, Japan interviews and concept testing, and drop first the extra key visuals, a pulse and one chapter refresh. We&#x27;d confirm the exact number in writing. Avoid A new number on the spot. 
 28 How do you work with our team day to day? Darcey Testing Collaboration 5. Answer Working sessions, not big reveals. A weekly status with Vanessa and Eric, shared timelines and trackers, and creative shown early enough to change. We&#x27;re minutes away, so in person is easy. Avoid Process jargon. 
 29 How do you bill? Brent Testing Transparency. Answer A signed estimate per phase, monthly invoices with hours by role, third-party costs at cost with the vendor invoice, and written change orders. No markup. Avoid Vague answers. 
 How to drill 
 Own questions (30 min). Filter to your name and answer every question out loud, timed. Anything over 60 seconds gets rewritten. 
 Mock (45 min). Teammates play Vanessa Chavez (Global Brand), Eric Lopez (product), Yash Simlote (strategy), Ai Phi Hahn (marketing) and Jackie Kan (digital). Always ask one follow-up. 
 Numbers lightning round (10 min). 25/20/15/15/10/10/5; $199,600 and $398,680; 976 and 1,702 hours; $125–$225; Nov 16 kickoff, April 2027 in market; Oct 21 approvals; 80% vs 30%, KCCQ +18; TMTT +47%. 
 11 Language guide 
 Say 
 operators, heart teams, imagers · TEER, M-TEER, T-TEER 
 “first choice for TEER,” “the default, not the alternative” 
 chapters, not flights 
 MLR, claims matrix, VAULT · “subject to Edwards medical, legal and regulatory review” 
 “one firm with the capabilities of many” 
 transcreated, verified · AI only with Edwards' approval 
 Don't 
 clinical claims or trial numbers beyond Edwards' published wording 
 knocking Abbott or MitraClip by name in creative (“confident without attacking anyone”) 
 “complex anatomy device” as our own description of PASCAL 
 “two companies” · overpromising specific results 
 other clients' confidential numbers 
 12 Watch-outs 
 45 minutes goes fast. Present in about 25 and hand the rest to them. Jeremy's territories are the part they'll remember, so protect that time. 
 Current facts. Tricuspid is now approved (Oct 21), and Capture Clarity is approved for degenerative mitral TEER in the US. Don't present them as future. 
 Results. They asked for case studies with results. Have one or two real numbers or client words ready. 
 Timeline mismatch in the RFP (Q2 2027 vs. end of 2027). Name it, don't assume. 
 Conflicts: none with Abbott or any TEER or mitral/tricuspid maker in 24 months. Our mitral valve-in-valve SAPIEN work is for Edwards. Say it plainly. 
 Advisory board: Kelly Fidler is an Edwards THV director. Confirm she's comfortable before we show her to TMTT. 
 Revel Republic appears on the agency overview slide as our events brand. That's fine for Edwards; just don't let it read as a second company.

 Mock interview × 
 Practice with Claude playing the committee 
 Claude asks questions in the style of the person you pick, pushes with follow-ups, and scores each answer. Answer by typing or out loud; a 60-second timer runs while you answer. Uses your own Claude usage. 
 Who's asking 
 Who's practicing Brent Jeremy Darcey Amanda Team 
 Difficulty Realistic Easy warm-up Hostile 
 Focus (optional) 
 How you'll answer Type Speak out loud 
 Answer out loud with your computer's built-in dictation 
 Mac: press the Fn / 🌐 key twice, or choose Edit → Start Dictation . First time: turn it on in System Settings → Keyboard → Dictation . 
 Windows: press Windows + H . First time, Windows may ask you to turn on online speech recognition. 
 Chromebook: Search + D . Phone or tablet: tap the microphone on the keyboard. 
 Click in the answer box, start dictation, and talk. Your words appear as you speak. Press the same keys again (or Esc) to stop. Dictation drops most "ums", so filler words aren't counted exactly; pace and length are. 
 Test it first. Click the box below, start dictation, and say: "We'll set a baseline for cost per application in the first thirty days." 
 Waiting for your words… Not working? Switch to Type . Everything else works the same.
